# BOOTCAMP VS SLOP DAWGZ

THE COMPLETE SEEDANCE 2.5 WORKFLOW · SHOT BY SHOT

The full build of the film, step by step: the exact prompts, the exact settings and the order everything was made in. All you need to do is build the references, then upload them into Seedance 2.5 omni-reference, alongside your prompt.

## STEP 1: BUILD THE CAST CHARACTER PASSPORTS

Before a single frame exists, every character is locked into one image, the character sheet. That image is the passport: the same person from every angle, in the same clothes, under the same light. From then on the passport is the character. It goes into every shot as a reference, and the model is left with nothing to invent.

| MODEL         | FORMAT | CHARACTERS |
| ------------- | ------ | ---------- |
| GPT Image 2.0 | 16:9   | 9          |

### BASIC PASSPORT

USE THIS PROMPT

Photorealistic character reference sheet: the SAME person repeated in multiple views across one seamless off-white studio background, arranged as a clean horizontal grid. Editorial lookbook photography, 85mm lens, soft even diffused frontal studio light, gentle contact shadow under the feet, no harsh shadows, neutral colour, ultra-realistic skin texture with visible pores and natural imperfections, razor-sharp focus, high-end fashion catalogue quality.

LAYOUT, left to right - every panel shows the same person in the same outfit:

1. Full-body FRONT view. Head to shoes fully inside the frame. Standing straight, arms relaxed at the sides, neutral expression, facing camera.
2. Full-body BACK view. Identical pose, showing the back of the garment.
3. Large MEDIUM shot, chest-up, three-quarter angle, natural relaxed posture.
4. Narrow right column of three stacked head-and-shoulders portraits: side profile, three-quarter angle, and frontal.

<!-- page 2 -->

CHARACTER - [age, build, ethnicity, hair, facial hair, skin, tattoos, scars, expression]

WARDROBE - [every garment, colour, material, logo and its exact placement, footwear, jewellery, headwear, eyewear]

Identical clothing, hair, jewellery and body proportions in every panel. Identical lighting in every panel. No text, no labels, no watermarks, no visible borders between panels.

Avoid: illustration, 3D render, plastic or waxy skin, airbrushed retouching, outfit changing between panels, cropped feet, duplicated limbs.

## EXTENDED PASSPORT

LABELLED VIEWS · SPECIFICATION PANELS · MACRO DETAIL

Photorealistic character passport sheet on a light neutral grey studio background, designed as a technical reference document with thin pale panel borders and small uppercase sans-serif labels.

TOP ROW, left to right:

* Large portrait panel - tight three-quarter head-and-shoulders shot

* Four evenly spaced full-body views labelled FRONT / BACK / SIDE / 3-4 VIEW, identical standing pose, head to shoes inside the frame

* Right column labelled FACE DETAILS - three stacked portraits captioned FRONT PORTRAIT, 3/4 PORTRAIT, SIDE PROFILE

BOTTOM ROW, four smaller panels:

* MATERIAL SPECIFICATIONS - list of garment materials and finishes

* [ACCESSORY] SPECIFICATIONS - the character's signature accessory shot alone on the same background

* FOOTWEAR DETAILS - the shoes alone from three angles

* DETAIL CLOSE-UPS - four macro crops: logo, seam, hardware, hem

Header top-left reads CHARACTER PASSPORT with a small subtitle line beneath.

Editorial studio photography, 85mm lens, soft even diffused light, ultra-realistic skin texture, razor-sharp focus, high-end catalogue quality.

CHARACTER - [age, build, ethnicity, hair, facial hair, skin, tattoos, scars, expression]

WARDROBE - [every garment, colour, material, logo and its exact placement, footwear, jewellery, headwear, eyewear]

Same lighting, same wardrobe, same proportions in every single panel.

FILL TWO SLOTS

CHARACTER

Age, build, height. Ethnicity. Hair colour, length and how it sits. Facial hair. Skin tone and texture. Tattoos and exactly where they fall on the body. Scars, piercings, teeth. Default expression.

<!-- page 3 -->

### WARDROBE

Every garment by name, colour and material. Logos and their exact placement: chest, back, sleeve. Footwear. Jewellery. Headwear. Eyewear. When the shape of a garment matters, describe the shape, not the brand.

THEN LOCK DOWN

### WARDROBE

Same garments, same colours, same logo placement in every shot of the film.

### HAIR

Same length, same parting, same styling or tie-back.

### SKIN

Tattoos in the same places, at the same size, in the same colours.

### JEWELLERY

Chains, rings, earrings. Either present in every shot or in none.

### EYEWEAR AND HEADWEAR

Decide once whether the character wears it. On in one shot and off in the next breaks the film faster than anything else.

### PROPORTIONS

Build and height never change. Left alone, the model makes everyone slimmer and taller.

<!-- page 4 -->

THE CAST OF THIS FILM

FINISHED PASSPORTS AS EXAMPLES

The two crews are dressed on separate palettes that never overlap: Bootcamp wears olive and orange, Slop Dawgz burgundy and sand. The burgundy bandana is already in every Slop Dawg passport, so it never has to be asked for in a shot prompt. Give your own groups a palette this strict and a viewer will tell them apart in a wide where no face is readable.

## STEP 2: LOCATIONS

The look of the film is decided here. Not in the character shots and not in the grade, but in the location: film stock, hour of day and sun direction are set once. That is why this section opens with the look block, copied into every location without a single edit and carried on into the shot prompts, every time. A location is a place, not a picture. The moment the camera moves, the model invents everything again unless you tell it where it now stands relative to the last view.

<!-- page 6 -->

| MODEL<br/>Nano Banana Pro | FORMAT<br/>16:9 | LOCATIONS<br/>6 |
| ------------------------- | --------------- | --------------- |

## VISUAL DNA

COPIED INTO EVERY LOCATION AND EVERY SHOT · NEVER EDITED

THE LOOK - paste this block into every location prompt and every shot prompt, word for word, never edited.

Shot on 35mm film, Kodak Vision3 500T, tungsten-balanced stock exposed in daylight through a warm 85 correction. Late golden hour, roughly forty minutes before sunset, the sun low at about ten degrees above the horizon. Warm golden-peach light on every surface facing the sun, cool pale blue-white sky, long soft shadows raking across the ground. Gentle halation blooming around the brightest highlights, fine organic grain, soft highlight roll-off, deep shadows that never crush to black. Natural colour, the way a real film stock renders a real street.

No teal-and-orange grading. No HDR. No digital clarity or sharpening. No lens flare. No dramatic clouds. Nothing wet unless it rained in the story.

## LOCATION MASTER PLATE

FIRST VIEW OF A LOCATION · LOOK BLOCK PLUS THE PLACE ITSELF

\=== LOOK - never changes, identical in every location ===

Shot on 35mm film, Kodak Vision3 500T, tungsten-balanced stock exposed in daylight through a warm 85 correction. Late golden hour, roughly forty minutes before sunset, the sun low at about ten degrees above the horizon. Warm golden-peach light on every surface facing the sun, cool pale blue-white sky, long soft shadows raking across the ground. Gentle halation blooming around the brightest highlights, fine organic grain, soft highlight roll-off, deep shadows that never crush to black. Natural colour, the way a real film stock renders a real street.

No teal-and-orange grading. No HDR. No digital clarity or sharpening. No lens flare. No dramatic clouds.

\=== PLACE - changes for every location ===

Photorealistic establishing plate of [WHAT THE PLACE IS]. [LENS]mm spherical lens, camera at standing eye level, perfectly level horizon, no tilt, no wide-angle distortion. The sun rakes in from [DIRECTION]. Documentary stillness, nobody in frame.

Foreground - [what is closest to the camera: the road surface, the kerb, the grass, the dust]

Midground - [the buildings, their material and colour, the vehicles, the fences]
Background - [what closes the frame at the far end]

Real-world imperfection: cracked asphalt, dry yellow grass, sun-faded paint, overhead

<!-- page 7 -->

power lines on wooden poles.

This must read as a photograph of a real street, not as a render.

Avoid: people, tilted horizon, oversaturation, staged tidiness.

## CHANGING THE LOCATION VIEWPOINT

FOR THE MAIN LOCATIONS · THE CAMERA DOES NOT MOVE, IT ONLY ROTATES

Same location as the reference image. The camera has not moved from its position, it has rotated [DEGREES] degrees to the [LEFT / RIGHT] and now faces [WHAT IS THERE].

KEEP IDENTICAL - the entire LOOK block, the sun in the same place in the sky, shadows falling in the same direction and at the same length, the same ground surface continuing underfoot, the same horizon height, the same lens, the same eye level.

WHAT STAYS VISIBLE - [the piece of the previous frame that must still be in view at the edge, so the two frames physically connect]

WHAT IS NEW - [what the rotation reveals]

Nobody in frame. This is one continuous place photographed from one standing position, not two different places.

## ANOTHER VIEW OF THE SAME PLACE

THE MASTER PLATE GOES IN AS REFERENCE · THE CAMERA CHANGES POSITION

The same location as in the reference image, photographed from a different camera position.

CAMERA - [where the camera now stands and which way it faces. For example: the camera has turned 180 degrees and now looks back out of the cul-de-sac towards the main road.]

KEEP IDENTICAL - the entire LOOK block, time of day, height and direction of the sun, colour and temperature of the light, length and direction of the shadows, architecture and its materials, road surface, vegetation, sky, film stock and grain. Same lens, same standing eye level, same level horizon.

NEW IN FRAME - [what becomes visible from this position that was behind the camera before]

Nobody in frame. Same documentary stillness. This is the same place at the same minute, only the camera has moved.

### FILL THREE SLOTS

#### WHERE THE CAMERA STANDS

Not "a view of the street" but a camera position: deep in the cul-de-sac, at the kerb, in the middle of the road. The model puts the camera exactly where your words put it.

#### WHICH WAY IT FACES

Direction matters more than content. If the camera turned 180 degrees, write that.

<!-- page 8 -->

### THE SUN

Height and direction. If shadows fall to the right in one frame, they fall to the right in all of them.

### TIME OF DAY

One hour for the whole location. Golden hour does not last forty minutes in life, but it does in a film.

### CAMERA HEIGHT

Eye level by default. Change it only when it is a decision, not an accident.

### LENS

One focal length per location. A different focal length reads as a different place.

### MATERIALS

Stucco, asphalt, chain-link, dry grass. List them once and repeat them verbatim.

### SKY

Colour and condition. Clouds appearing break a location harder than a moved building.

### EMPTINESS

Not one accidental person. People appear only where the story puts them.

Guys, think like a cinematographer on real film-set rather than someone ordering a picture. A cinematographer never says "show me the street"; he says "camera is here, facing that way, the exit is behind us".

## VEHICLES AND PROPS <span style="float: right; font-size: small; font-weight: normal; color: grey;">OBJECT PASSPORTS</span>

You need to protect the objects. A viewer will not notice that the bat got longer, but they will stop believing the shot. If you are putting an object into more than one scene, you need an object passport.

| MODEL         | FORMAT | OBJECTS | SHEETS |
| ------------- | ------ | ------- | ------ |
| GPT Image 2.0 | 4:3    | 15      | 17     |

### OBJECT PASSPORT

Photorealistic object reference sheet on one seamless pale grey studio background, arranged as a clean two-row grid. Product photography, 85mm lens, soft even diffused studio light from above and slightly front, gentle contact shadow under the object, no harsh reflections, neutral colour, razor-sharp focus, high-end catalogue quality.

TOP ROW - six orthographic views of the same object, evenly spaced, identical scale, each with a small uppercase sans-serif label beneath:
FRONT / FRONT 3-4 LEFT / LEFT SIDE / RIGHT SIDE / REAR 3-4 RIGHT / REAR

BOTTOM ROW - five macro detail panels, each labelled, showing the parts that make this object recognisable: [name the five details that matter for your object, for example logo, seam, hardware, texture, worn edge]

OBJECT - [what it is, its exact material and finish, colour, scale relative to a human, condition and wear, any marking or logo and its exact placement]

<!-- page 13 -->

Identical object, identical finish and identical lighting in every panel. Nothing else in frame. No people, no props, no environment, no background scenery.

Avoid: dramatic rim light, coloured gels, reflections of a studio, floating shadowless object, plastic CGI look.

## OBJECT STATE

THE BASE PASSPORT GOES IN AS REFERENCE · ONLY THE STATE CHANGES

The same object as in the reference sheet, in a different state.

KEEP IDENTICAL - the sheet layout, the seamless pale grey background, the soft even light, the contact shadow, the camera angles, the scale, the position and style of the labels.

WHAT CHANGES - [the new state: new paint, new material, new configuration, new fittings]

WHAT MUST NOT CHANGE - [the geometry, proportions and identifying details that prove it is still the same object]

Both sheets must be able to sit side by side and read as one object photographed twice, not as two objects.

FILL THREE SLOTS

THE OBJECT

What it is, what it is made of, its size relative to a person, its condition. Wear and use matter more than shine: a brand-new object always looks like a render.

FIVE DETAILS

Name the five macro panels your object needs. On a bike: bars, weld, crank, wheel, saddle. On a ring: face, setting, shoulder, inner band. The model will not guess what matters to you.

MARKINGS

Logo, number, lettering and exactly where they sit. This is what lets a viewer recognise the object in a shot faster than its shape does.

LOCK ACROSS ALL OBJECTS

BACKGROUND AND LIGHT

The same seamless pale grey and the same soft light on every object in the film. A different background turns a set of passports into a set of borrowed pictures.

SCALE

An object has to look at its real size. The ring and the Impala are shot the same way, but they must never end up the same height in frame.

<!-- page 14 -->

## BOOTCAMP 09

MARKINGS

The Bootcamp logo sits on the bat, the BMX frame, the Impala wing, the bike tank, the piano and the signet ring. For Slop Dawgz the burgundy and the bandana paisley do the same job: it carries onto the skate deck, the katana wrap, the blaster body and the side of the van. A crew is recognised by its objects before its faces.

WEAR

One level of wear per object for the whole film. Scratched in one scene and factory-fresh in another are two different objects.

EMPTINESS

No environment, no hands, no stands, no styling. The object alone.

# THE FILM, SHOT BY SHOT SEEDANCE 2.5 · 72 GENERATIONS

Now that you have your character sheets, locations, and object sheets. You just need to upload them into Seedance 2.5 Omni-reference (Up to 50 references at once), alongside your prompts. You can copy and paste the prompts listed below into an AI chat and ask it to structure all of the prompts for your film in the same way.

## SHOT 01 14s

SCENE 1 · COLD OPEN

One unbroken take with no camera move at all.

BOOT STREET

HOW THE PROMPT IS BUILT

`01 STOCK AND LOOK`

`02 SCENE CONTEXT`

`03 REFS @1-@8`

`05 CAMERA`

`06 STAGING`

## FULL PROMPT

14S · ONE TAKE · 8 REFERENCES

35mm Kodak 500T. handheld dynamic cinematography. Low golden-hour sun, warm skin tones, cool skylight on the shadow side. Photorealistic live-action, raw documentary realism. No 3D render, no game engine, no plastic skin, no retouch, no oversharpening, no HDR grading. 24fps, 180-degree shutter blur. SFX only no music. No subtitles. American accent. 14s. Scene context: A quiet LA cul-de-sac at golden hour, a crew of creators on their block. The man with the headband films himself on his own small camera, showing off to the lens. The blonde woman across the street undercuts him without raising her voice. Nobody travels anywhere. @image1 - Black man, black paisley headband, full black beard, olive bomber with orange BC monogram, white tee, silver chain, baggy dark jeans. Neck and chest tattoos read as dark texture only, never a legible drawing. @image2 - his BMX: matte dark-green scuffed frame, black parts, 4 pegs. @image3 - blonde woman, dark roots, high long ponytail, narrow silver-framed smoke-tinted sunglasses, hoops, olive knit crop top with orange BC monogram, denim shorts. @image4 - the car: graphite low coupe, long hood, fastback roof, chrome multi-spoke wheels. @image5 - East Asian man, black hair in a low ponytail, colour irezumi neck collar, small fox tattoo on right cheekbone, same olive bomber. @Image6 - motorcycle: glossy black supersport, orange pinstripes and rim tape. @image7 - silver 1980s cassette boombox, round speakers, orange tuner stripe. @image8 - location only: LA cul-de-sac, cream and sand stucco houses, tall bare-trunk palms, cracked asphalt, poles and wires, dry lawns. All cards give appearance ONLY. Do NOT reproduce sheet layout, multiple views, panels, captions, grey backdrop or catalogue pose. STRICT: EXACTLY THREE PEOPLE. @image1 = ONLY black headband and beard. @image3 = ONLY blonde ponytail and tinted glasses. @image5 = ONLY ponytail and tattooed neck. 35mm Kodak 500T. handheld dynamic cinematography. Low golden-hour sun, warm skin tones, cool skylight on shadow side. Photorealistic live-action, raw documentary realism. No 3D render, no game engine, no plastic skin. 24fps, 180-degree shutter blur. SFX only no music. No subtitles. American accent. 16s.

Scene context: An LA cul-de-sac at golden hour. The blonde woman lands the last word on the man filming himself. He gives up answering, sets his camera down on the asphalt, picks up his BMX and rides straight at it. The two across the street stay busy throughout.
@image1 - Black man, black paisley headband, full beard, olive bomber with orange BC, white tee, silver chain, baggy jeans. Neck tattoos as dark texture.

@image2 - BMX: matte dark-green scuffed frame, black parts, 4 pegs.

@image3 - blonde woman, dark roots, high ponytail, narrow silver smoke-tinted sunglasses, hoops, olive crop top with orange BC, denim shorts.

@image4 - East Asian man, low ponytail, irezumi neck collar, small fox tattoo on right cheek, olive bomber.

@image5 - moto: glossy black supersport, orange pinstripes and rim tape.

@image6 - silver 1980s boombox, round speakers, orange tuner stripe.

@image7 - THE LOCATION AND THE EXACT DIRECTION THE CAMERA IS FACING. Use it for everything: an LA cul-de-sac at golden hour, cream and sand stucco bungalows, very tall bare-trunk palms, cracked pale asphalt turning circle, poles and wires, dry yellow lawns, a basketball hoop at the right. At the LEFT of frame, parked nose-out on a driveway, sits a GRAPHITE LOW-SLUNG SPORTS COUPE with chrome wheels. Ordinary neighbourhood cars are parked along the kerbs around the circle - they are scenery only, nobody is in them and none of them ever move.

All cards give appearance ONLY. Do NOT reproduce sheet layout, multiple views, panels, captions or grey backdrop.

STRICT: EXACTLY THREE PEOPLE. @image1 = ONLY black headband and beard. @image3 = ONLY blonde ponytail and tinted glasses. @image4 = ONLY ponytail and tattooed neck. Never duplicate or add a fourth. No neighbours, no pedestrians, no extras.

CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS. We look THROUGH this camera, never at it: its body is NEVER visible in frame at any point.

0.0-5.1s: in @image1's RIGHT hand at arm's length, selfie framing, 22mm, gentle barrel distortion. Live handheld weight: vertical bobbing with his breathing, lateral sway, roll tilts, micro-jitter, corrections keeping his face in frame.

5.1-7.3s: he lowers it and the framing falls with his hand - street, kerb, then asphalt swinging up through frame as the lens descends, still shaking.

7.3-16.0s: it rests ON THE GROUND, 25cm up, tilted slightly up, looking down the street. From this instant COMPLETELY DEAD STILL - no breathing, drift, float or jitter, because nobody holds it.

STAGING: @image2 lies FLAT ON ITS SIDE on the asphalt near him. AT THE LEFT of frame, the graphite coupe sits parked on its driveway exactly as in @image7, engine off, wheels never turning. @image3 sits UP ON ITS ROOF, one knee up, the other leg down the side of the door, a wooden bat standing VERTICALLY in her left hand, knob resting by her hip. @image6 sits on the ground by its front wheel. @image4 leans against the front wing of the same car, arms folded. @image5 stands behind him on its side stand.

BACKGROUND LIFE - NEVER FROZEN, ESPECIALLY ONCE THE CAMERA IS DOWN. From 7.3s on, @image3 and @image4 sit at the LEFT EDGE of frame by the parked coupe, soft and out of focus, both moving throughout. @image3 chews nonstop, her hanging leg swings and taps the door, she shifts her seat, regrips the bat, pushes hair off her face, turns to watch him. @image4 shifts weight hip to hip, unfolds and refolds his arms, pushes off the wing and settles back. Hair and clothes move in the breeze. Neither ever holds a pose or goes statue-still.
ACTION, TIMED:

0.0-2.6s @image3, calm and flat, into the lens: "Then he should be terrified. This is me being nice." Throughout, @image1's MOUTH STAYS CLOSED - lips together, jaw still, not one

word shaped. He only listens; he never mouths, echoes or lip-syncs her line.

2.6-3.6s Only now does @image1 open his mouth to answer, and finds nothing. Jaw works once, brows up and down. He closes it. Beaten and aware of it.

3.6-4.8s In depth @image3 blows a SMALL pink bubble, about fist-sized, no wider than her mouth.

4.8-5.1s It POPS. His brows flick up at the sound.

6.3-7.3s He sets it on the asphalt. His fingers pass HUGE and out of focus across the lens as he lets go - we stay inside the camera.

7.3-8.0s His hand leaves frame. The image locks: a low dead-still wide of the empty street.

8.0-9.6s He straightens, walks away down the street, bends and hauls @image2 up by the bars.

9.6-11.2s He swings onto it and cranks hard, small in depth, coming back toward the lens. @image4 turns his head to follow.

11.2-14.2s He accelerates STRAIGHT AT the camera, growing fast in frame, cranks driving, tyres roaring.

14.2-15.0s At the last moment he BUNNY HOPS - the BMX rises and passes directly over the lens, frame filling with the dark underside of the bike and sky between tyres.

15.0-16.0s He is gone past. Empty street, dust settling, landing heard behind camera. @image3 and @image4 still moving at the left edge.

Every beat lands on the tail of the one before. No dead air, no waiting. Speech is never sped up or compressed.

ONE LINE IS SPOKEN AND ONLY @image3 SPEAKS IT. @image1 and @image4 say nothing; their lips never move to words. SFX - wind past the mic, distant traffic, palm fronds, a small sharp bubblegum POP, the camera knocking onto grit, footsteps, the BMX lifted off the road, chain and freewheel, tyres roaring, a hard landing behind camera. Boombox thin across the street.

Once the camera is down it never moves again - no handheld breathing after 7.3s, no drift, reframing, pan, tilt or push. No slow motion or speed ramp; the hop plays full speed. No cuts. The BMX never spins in the air, no 180. NOTHING ELSE DRIVES OR ROLLS: every parked car in the street stays parked, engines off, wheels never turning; the moto never moves. @image3 never leaves the roof of the coupe. @image4 never walks off. The block simply exists. @Image1 - Black man, black paisley headband, full beard, olive bomber with orange BC, white tee, baggy dark jeans. @image2 - BMX: matte dark-green scuffed frame, black parts, 4 pegs. @image3 - blonde woman, dark roots, high ponytail, narrow silver smoke-tinted sunglasses, olive crop top with orange BC, denim shorts. @image4 - car: graphite low coupe, long hood, fastback roof, chrome wheels. @image5 - East Asian man, low ponytail, irezumi neck collar, olive bomber. @image6 - moto: glossy black supersport, orange pinstripes and rim tape. @image7 - silver 1980s boombox, round speakers, orange tuner stripe. @image8 - location only: LA cul-de-sac, cream and sand stucco houses, very tall bare-trunk palms, cracked pale asphalt, wooden poles and wires, dry yellow lawns, basketball hoop. No pedestrians, no neighbours, no extras, no duplicates. CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS. Extreme long shot, 28mm, camera at chest height, held across the street in profile so the whole cul-de-sac reads side-on. The camera TRACKS SLOWLY AND STEADILY FROM FRAME-LEFT TO FRAME-RIGHT across the full 8 seconds at a constant unhurried pace, with the soft live weight of a shoulder rig underneath it - gentle breathing, small roll, never mechanical, never perfectly smooth. It never stops, never speeds up, never pushes in. THE MOVE IS PROVEN BY PARALLAX: near palm trunks sweep across the frame quickly, mid-ground palms and poles cross more slowly, the houses and the far end of the street drift almost imperceptibly. Foreground trunks pass between camera and subjects and briefly occlude them. STAGING - everything except the BMX is stationary and stays exactly where it is. @image4 is PARKED at the kerb on the left of the street, engine off, wheels not turning. @image3 sits up on its roof, one knee up, other leg hanging down the door; a light wooden baseball bat stands VERTICALLY in her left hand, its knob resting on the roof beside her hip. A white baseball rests in her right hand. @image7 sits on the ground by the front wheel, playing. @image5 leans against the front wing, arms folded, weight on one hip. @image6 stands further back on its side stand. Long palm shadows lie in stripes across the asphalt; the low sun rakes the house fronts warm while cool skylight fills the shadow side of every figure and separates the olive clothing from the sand-coloured walls. ACTION, CONTINUOUS FOR ALL 8 SECONDS - no beats, no events, just simultaneous life: @image1 carves wide lazy circles on @image2 out on the open asphalt of the turning circle, weight rocking, cranking a few strokes then coasting, front wheel lifting into a short manual and dropping again, riding in and out from behind the foreground palms. @image3 chews pink gum slowly and without stopping, her hanging leg swinging loose, turning the baseball over in her right hand, shifting her seat on the roof, her head slowly following him as he passes. @image5 shifts

It moves laterally along the street and STOPS TWICE. Each stop is a slow deceleration easing into rest over about half a second, never an abrupt halt, and each restart eases back into motion just as gradually. THE MOVE IS PROVEN BY PARALLAX throughout: near palm trunks sweep past fast, mid-ground poles cross slower, far houses drift almost imperceptibly. Foreground trunks pass between camera and subjects and briefly occlude them. ACTION, TIMED: 0.0-3.0s The camera travels FRAME-LEFT TO FRAME-RIGHT alongside @image1, holding him near as he cranks and coasts across the turning circle. 35mm Kodak 500T. handheld dynamic cinematography. Low golden-hour sun, warm skin tones, cool skylight on shadow side. Photorealistic live-action, raw documentary realism. No 3D render, no game engine, no plastic skin. 24fps, 180-degree shutter blur. SFX only no music. No subtitles. American accent. 20s. Scene context: A quiet LA cul-de-sac at golden hour. A big man comes out of his front door mid-phone-call, down the steps, past the car on his driveway and out onto the street, still talking. Annoyed, businesslike, unhurried. The camera walks backwards in front of him and the street opens up behind his shoulders. One unbroken take - the comedy lives in never cutting it. @image1 - very large heavyset Black man, black bowler hat, thick black-rimmed glasses, clear lenses, short greying beard, mole on left cheek, olive short-sleeve shirt with orange BC on the pocket, baggy jeans. @image2 - phone: black 1980s brick handset, long rubber antenna, grey keypad, scuffed. @image3 - HIS HOUSE, start of shot only: peach stucco bungalow, brown shingled

roof, white metal security screen door with scrollwork, low concrete steps, driveway with a silver sedan parked on it, green shrubs, tall palm trunk right. @image4 - THE STREET he walks into: same cul-de-sac, cream stucco houses, tall bare-trunk palms, cracked asphalt, poles, dry lawns. All cards give appearance ONLY. Do NOT reproduce sheet layout, multiple views, panels, captions or grey backdrop. STRICT: EXACTLY ONE PERSON IN THE WHOLE SHOT. @image1 = ONLY bowler hat and heavy build. The street behind him is completely empty of people: no crew, no neighbours, no pedestrians, no extras, no duplicates. CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS, NO SECOND ANGLE. 35mm, chest height, shoulder-mounted handheld walking BACKWARDS in front of him for all 20 seconds, retreating at exactly his pace so he stays the same size in frame. Real operator weight: a soft vertical step-bounce per stride, small roll, tiny reframing corrections, never smooth or mechanical. He fills the frame chest-up and always faces the lens. As the camera retreats his house falls away behind him and THE STREET opens up BEHIND HIS SHOULDERS - first driveway and palm, then house fronts, then the far end of the empty cul-de-sac. Parallax proves the move: the near palm sweeps past, poles cross slower, far houses barely drift. STAGING: @image1 holds @image2 to his right ear the entire take, left hand loose, gesturing once or twice. The silver sedan sits PARKED on his driveway, engine off, wheels never turning; he walks around it. Ordinary neighbourhood cars are parked along the kerbs in depth - scenery only, nobody in them, none of them ever move. ACTION, TIMED: 0.0-2.0s The white security screen door swings open and @image1 comes out, phone already at his ear, mid-conversation, and starts down the concrete steps. He is walking before we ever see him still. 2.0-3.2s @image1, flat and final: "Sunset. Under the bridge." 3.2-4.7s He listens, moving down the driveway past the sedan, straight at the lens. Jaw setting. 4.7-10.1s @image1, hard and irritated, building: "Tell that smug punk Johnny to get himself down there - and bring the prototype he stole like the little rat he is." 10.1-11.3s He listens, brows drawing together. He shakes his head once, slowly, and steps off the driveway onto the street. 11.3-13.7s @image1, tired and pointed: "And don't let that clown Mumbles write the address down." 13.7-14.7s He listens, then his eyes close for a beat and open again - remembering something he wishes he could forget. 14.7-17.3s @image1, dry and weary, almost to himself: "Last time I gave him four digits, he came back with six." 17.3-20.0s He hangs up with his thumb, drops @image2 to his hip and keeps walking at the lens, the whole street now open behind him. ACTING: @image1's eyes are fully visible behind clear lenses; he never performs to camera. AU4 furrow deepening through the second line, nostrils flaring once on "stole", jaw working, AU17 chin raise on the Mumbles line, then flat weariness on the last. ONLY @image1 SPEAKS AND ONLY THE LINES ABOVE. The person on the other end is NEVER heard - no voice from the phone, no audio leak, no reply. No ad-libs, mumbling, grunts, unscripted sighs or breaths-as-voice, chuckles or offscreen voices. Each line lands on the tail of the beat before it; no dead air, no waiting, speech never sped up or compressed. SFX - the screen door swinging shut behind him, footsteps on concrete then asphalt, wind past the mic, dry palm fronds, a distant BMX freewheel, far traffic. STRICT NEGATIVES: no cut, insert, reverse angle, close-up of the phone, shot of whoever he is talking to, flashback, or imagery of Johnny or Mumbles - they are audio only and never appear. The camera never stops, overtakes him, circles him or gets behind his shoulder. No tripod, gimbal, dolly, crane or drone. No zoom, push-in or rack focus. No slow motion or speed ramp. The silver sedan never moves, wheels never turn; no parked car in the street ever moves. No relighting or change of time of day.

## FULL PROMPT

6S · ONE TAKE · 2 REFERENCES

85mm Kodak 500T. cinematography. Low golden-hour sun, warm haze, cool skylight in the shadows. Photorealistic live-action, raw documentary realism. No 3D render, no game engine, no CGI sheen. 24fps, 180-degree shutter. SFX only no music. No subtitles. 6s. Scene context: The same LA cul-de-sac at golden hour, looking straight down the length of the street. A black 1960s coupe has turned in at the far end and is coming this way, unhurried. Nobody is in this shot. It is just the car arriving, seen on a long lens, with the whole street stacked up flat behind it. @image1 - the car, the ONLY subject in frame: 1964 black hardtop coupe, long flat hood, thin chrome side spear, quad round tail lights, chrome bumpers, wire wheels with whitewall tyres, a small white logo on the rear quarter panel, sitting low. @Image2 - location only: LA cul-de-sac, cream and sand stucco houses, very tall bare-trunk palms, cracked pale asphalt, wooden poles and wires, dry yellow lawns, kerbs. All cards give appearance ONLY. Do NOT reproduce sheet layout, multiple views, panels, captions, grey studio backdrop or studio floor. STRICT: NOBODY IS IN THIS SHOT. No people, no pedestrians, no figures on the street, no driver visible through the

BOOTCAMP VS SLOP DAWGZ 33

<!-- page 34 -->

glass, no other moving vehicles. The only thing that moves is this one car. CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS. Long lens, 85mm, shoulder-mounted, positioned low in the middle of the road and looking straight down the street on the car's centreline. The heavy glass damps the handheld into a slow, wide, lazy float - big soft drift rather than fast jitter, with an occasional small correction as the operator keeps the car in the middle of frame. The camera NEVER pans, tilts, zooms, pushes in or moves along the street. It stays where it is and lets the car come to it. THE LONG LENS COMPRESSES EVERYTHING: the palms, poles and parked cars along both kerbs are stacked flat on top of each other with almost no depth between them, the far end of the street looks as close as the near end, and the background barely shifts at all as the car advances. ACTION, TIMED: 0.0-1.6s The car sits SMALL and far away at the end of the street, already rolling toward us, its shape softened by warm haze and heat shimmer coming off the asphalt. 1.6-4.0s It comes on steadily at a constant unhurried speed, growing in the frame - first the whole car readable, then the front end filling more and more of the width, chrome catching the low sun in one long moving highlight along the flank. 4.0-6.0s It fills the centre of frame from bumper to roofline, still coming, still level, close enough now that the grille and the chrome bumper read sharply. It never reaches the camera and never stops. The car is unhurried and completely level throughout - no acceleration, no braking, no swerving, no drifting, no engine revving. MOTION IS PROVEN BY THE GROUND, NOT ASSERTED: the cracked asphalt and its painted marks slide steadily underneath and past the bottom of frame, the kerbs creep in from both sides, the wire wheels turn visibly and their spokes blur into flat spinning discs, the whitewalls read as clean bright rings, and the car's own hard shadow travels along the road with it. The body rocks very slightly on its soft old suspension over the uneven surface. SFX - a deep, low, unhurried V8 idle growing steadily louder and closer through the whole shot, tyres rolling on cracked asphalt, dry palm fronds, wind past the mic, far traffic. No music. STRICT NEGATIVES: no people anywhere in frame. No cut, insert, second angle, close-up or reverse. The camera never pans, tilts, whips, zooms, racks focus, pushes in, tracks alongside or drives with the car. No tripod lock, no gimbal glide, no dolly, no crane, no drone, no aerial, no low hero angle from the ground. No slow motion, no speed ramp, no bullet time. The car never spins its wheels, never smokes the tyres, never lights up, never revs, never hops. The wheels never appear frozen, reversed or strobing. The street behind never slides sideways as one flat backdrop. Nothing changes colour. No relighting or change of time of day.

35mm Kodak 500T. handheld dynamic cinematography. Low golden-hour sun, warm skin tones, cool skylight on shadow side. Photorealistic live-action, raw documentary realism. No 3D render, no game engine, no plastic skin. 24fps, 180-degree shutter. SFX only no music. No subtitles. American accent. 13s.

@image1 - THE ARRIVING MAN, late twenties, olive skin, black hair with volume on top and a short skin fade, dense full black beard, glossy ORANGE wraparound cat-eye sunglasses with amber-brown lenses worn throughout, BLACK track jacket with curved off-white panels and a small burnt-orange logo, white tee, oversized black trousers with a white stripe, white sneakers.

@image2 - HIS CAR: 1964 BLACK hardtop coupe, long flat hood, chrome bumpers and side spear, quad round tail lights, wire wheels with whitewalls, sitting low. THE DRIVER'S WINDOW IS FULLY WOUND DOWN for the whole shot.

white tee, silver chain. Neck tattoos as dark texture. Closest person to camera, clearly readable at all times.

@image4 - very large heavyset Black man, black bowler hat, thick black-rimmed glasses with clear lenses, short greying beard, olive short-sleeve shirt with orange BC, a black brick phone in one hand.

@image5 - blonde woman, dark roots, high ponytail, narrow silver smoke-tinted sunglasses, olive crop top with orange BC, denim shorts, a light wooden baseball bat. She chews gum continuously.

@image6 - East Asian man, low ponytail, irezumi neck collar, small fox tattoo on right cheek, olive bomber with orange BC, white tee.

@image7 - THE LOCATION AND THE EXACT DIRECTION THE CAMERA IS FACING. Use it for everything, including the vehicles already in it: an LA cul-de-sac at golden hour, cream stucco bungalows around a cracked asphalt circle, tall bare-trunk palms, poles and wires, dry lawns. AT THE LEFT, parked nose-out on a driveway, a GRAPHITE LOW-SLUNG SPORTS COUPE with chrome wheels; a BLACK SUPERSPORT MOTORCYCLE on its side stand behind it; a SILVER BOOMBOX on the ground beside the coupe. All other cars are scenery only.

All character cards give appearance ONLY. Do NOT reproduce sheet layout, multiple views, panels, captions or backdrop.

STRICT: EXACTLY FIVE PEOPLE, ALL FIVE VISIBLE AND READABLE. @image1 = ONLY orange sunglasses and black tracksuit. @image3 = ONLY black headband and beard. @image4 = ONLY bowler hat and heavy build. @image5 = ONLY blonde ponytail and tinted glasses. @image6 = ONLY low ponytail and tattooed neck. No extras or duplicates. @image2 is the ONLY vehicle that moves.

WHERE EVERYONE IS: @image3 and @image4 stand TOGETHER OUT ON THE OPEN ASPHALT in the right half of frame, well clear of any vehicle and NOT leaning on anything. @image3 is the nearest person to camera and stays fully visible for the whole shot; @image4 stands beside him, slightly further back. Both are already looking OFF FRAME RIGHT at an approaching engine. SEPARATELY, over at the LEFT and AT THE GRAPHITE COUPE, @image5 sits UP ON the car with one leg hanging down the door and the bat upright in her hand, and @image6 leans back against its front wing with his arms folded; the boombox is on the ground beside it. A matte dark-green BMX LIES FLAT ON ITS SIDE on the asphalt and nobody picks it up.

WHERE THE CAR COMES FROM AND WHERE IT STOPS: @image2 enters FROM FRAME RIGHT - the same direction everyone is looking - and stops in open asphalt so that it NEVER blocks @image3 or @image4 from camera. It does not come from the left, from behind camera or from a driveway.

CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS, NO SECOND ANGLE. 35mm, CHEST HEIGHT, shoulder-mounted handheld with soft operator weight - gentle float, small roll, tiny corrections, never smooth. It stands off to one side with the crew already in frame and lets the car arrive into that space. It reframes slightly as he gets out, then holds, keeping @image1 and @image3 both in frame together. It never rises, drops, pushes in or circles.

ACTION, TIMED:

0.0-2.8s @image2 rolls in from frame right at walking speed, unhurried, and stops softly in the open space among them, settling on its suspension. The engine cuts. The driver's window is down.

2.8-4.4s The driver's door swings open. @image1 gets out in one ordinary movement, stands, and PUSHES THE DOOR PROPERLY SHUT behind him with a solid thunk. The window stays open. No pause, no posing.

4.4-5.8s @image3, looking at the car and not at him, dry: "Dan, you ever gonna put a color grade on that thing?"

5.8-6.8s @image1 does not answer. One corner of his mouth lifts into a small dry SMIRK, held briefly, and settles again. That is his entire reply.

6.8-9.6s @image5, flat, from up on the coupe, not looking at anyone: "Leave it. It's a classic. Suits him."

9.6-10.8s @image1 turns his HEAD toward her and holds a moment. Then he turns his head back toward his own car.

10.8-13.0s WITHOUT REOPENING THE DOOR, he reaches IN THROUGH THE OPEN DRIVER'S WINDOW, takes something dark and heavy off the seat, and draws it back out through the window, straightening with it in his hand.

ACTING: @image1's eyes are completely hidden behind amber lenses, so ALL of his expression lives in the brows above the frames, the nostrils, the jaw and cheek, the mouth, and in slow deliberate head movements - he changes what he looks at by turning his head, never by moving his eyes. The smirk at 5.8s is small and dry, never a grin, and it is the only thing he gives anyone. @image3's eyes are visible with uneven blinking, easy and teasing. @image5 plays entirely in mouth and chin behind tinted lenses; completely deadpan, never smiles. @image4 and @image6 simply watch. Identity and skin texture stay constant.

NEVER FROZEN: all four of the crew stay continuously alive from the first frame - @image5 chews nonstop and her hanging leg swings, @image6 shifts weight and refolds his arms, @image3 and @image4 shift their footing and turn their heads to follow the car. Hair and clothing move in the breeze. Nobody holds a pose.

ONLY @image3 AND @image5 SPEAK, AND ONLY THE LINES ABOVE. @image1 SAYS NOTHING AT ALL - his lips never move to words and he never mouths or echoes anyone. @image4 and @image6 never speak. No ad-libs, mumbling, grunts, breaths-as-voice, chuckles or laughs. Each line lands on the tail of the beat before; no dead air, speech never sped up.

SFX - a deep V8 idle rolling in from the right and shutting off, tyres crunching on grit, a heavy door swinging open and closing with a solid thunk, footsteps, fabric brushing the door as he reaches through the window, wind past the mic, palm fronds, a faint boombox nearby.

STRICT NEGATIVES: @image3 IS NEVER HIDDEN, blocked by the car, cropped out of frame or lost behind anyone - he stays fully visible from first frame to last. @image3 and @image4 NEVER lean on, touch or stand next to any vehicle - they stay out on open asphalt the whole shot. @image5 and @image6 NEVER leave the graphite coupe. NO HERO ENTRANCE: no slow motion, speed ramp, low hero angle, camera rising to meet him, push-in on his face, lens flare timed to his arrival or backlight halo. @image2 enters ONLY from frame right and never revs, spins its wheels, smokes the tyres or hops. THE DOOR IS OPENED ONLY ONCE and after he closes it he NEVER opens it again - the object comes out through the open window and only through the window. The window is never wound up. Nobody applauds, greets him, steps forward or defers. @image1 never speaks, never grins broadly, never removes his sunglasses. No cut, insert, reverse angle or close-up. No tripod, gimbal, dolly, crane or drone. No zoom or rack focus. @image3 never picks the BMX up. @image5 never leaves the coupe and never puts the bat down. @image6 never unfolds his arms. NOTHING ELSE DRIVES OR ROLLS: the graphite coupe stays parked with wheels still, the motorcycle stays on its stand. Nobody touches the boombox. No relighting or change of time of day.

On the LOCATION'S RIGHT are a parked dark-grey pickup and a basketball hoop. On the LOCATION'S LEFT are a parked graphite Dodge Viper, black motorcycle and silver boombox.

The RED ARROW on @image1 is ONLY an off-screen trajectory guide. It is never visible in the video. Reconstruct clean natural cracked asphalt beneath it: no red line, paint, marking, glow or reflection.

@image2 - THE ONLY MOVING CAR: a 1964 BLACK Chevrolet Impala hardtop, long flat hood, glossy black paint, chrome bumpers and side trim, low natural stance, chrome wire wheels and narrow whitewall tyres. Preserve its exact proportions, wheels, paint, chrome and ride height. The reference sheet gives appearance ONLY; never reproduce its panels, labels, captions, studio backdrop or multiple views.

STRICT: one moving Impala only, no duplicate. No people. Every vehicle already in @image1 remains parked and motionless.

ROUTE - steering directions are from the DRIVER'S perspective: @image2 enters through the BOTTOM-RIGHT street opening where the red path begins. It follows the broad RIGHT-HAND OUTER ARC of the circular road instead of cutting straight across the centre. The front wheels initially steer RIGHT, smoothly unwind through centre, then progressively steer

LEFT as the car rounds the circle and travels leftward across the far side. One continuous flowing S-curve, no sharp change or diagonal shortcut.

CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS OR SECOND ANGLE. A low damped exterior tracking arm follows the DRIVER-SIDE FRONT-LEFT WHEEL at wheel-hub height. It is attached to the car body, never to the spinning wheel; the camera does not rotate with the rim. 50mm close-up. The complete wheel, whitewall, chrome spokes, lower fender and wheel arch fill 55-65 percent of frame height. Never reveal the full car.

The wheel stays slightly left of centre at nearly constant size while asphalt and houses move behind it with correct continuous parallax. Small road vibration, gentle vertical float and tiny organic corrections; tactile, never perfectly mechanical. Focus stays locked on the tyre, spokes and wheel arch. Moderately shallow depth of field: the background is soft but the pickup and basketball hoop remain recognizable. No rack focus.
CRITICAL CAMERA DIRECTION: the camera travels on the INSIDE/DRIVER'S SIDE of the route and always looks across the wheel toward the RIGHT-HAND SIDE of @image1. The dark-grey pickup and basketball hoop occupy the upper/right background. Subtle pan compensation keeps this world-space direction consistent through the curve. The camera never looks toward the location's left side. The Dodge Viper, motorcycle and boombox remain physically behind camera and NEVER appear, including at frame edges or in the Impala's reflections.

ACTION, TIMED:

0.0-1.7s The shot begins already tight on the rotating FRONT-LEFT wheel as the Impala enters from bottom-right at a controlled 12-15 km/h. The wheel is visibly steered RIGHT. Rotation exactly matches forward movement. During the right-hand curve, weight transfers subtly to the car's LEFT: the visible front-left suspension compresses slightly and the fender settles closer to the tyre.

1.7-3.3s The steering smoothly unwinds through straight and progressively turns LEFT. The Impala follows the marked arc without sliding. Weight transfers gently toward the RIGHT side, so the visible front-left suspension unloads slightly and the wheel-arch gap increases by a small believable amount. The pickup and hoop move through the soft background with physically correct parallax.

3.3-5.0s The wheel returns close to straight as the Impala continues leftward. A crack in the asphalt produces one small damped compression and rebound. The take ends while the car is still rolling, before the left-side Viper area could enter view.

VEHICLE PHYSICS: the tyre remains planted with a subtle flattened contact patch. No slipping, floating or reverse rotation. Wire spokes show natural 24fps motion blur while their design stays constant; the whitewall remains perfectly circular and unchanged. The wheel follows small asphalt height changes while the heavy body floats independently above it. Correct restrained body roll, one damped rebound, no repeated bouncing. Glossy paint and chrome carry continuous warm moving reflections without warping or revealing off-camera objects. The car's shadow stays attached to the tyres.

SFX - restrained deep V8 under light load, tyres over cracked asphalt, faint grit, one subdued suspension creak, slight vintage body-and-chrome rattle, wind past the low microphone, distant palm fronds. No dialogue.

STRICT NEGATIVES: NO RED ARROW. No wide or establishing shot, no full-car reveal, no cut, insert, second angle, zoom, rack focus, orbit or camera crossing the front of the car. No Viper, motorcycle or boombox, even briefly or in reflections. No people, duplicate Impala or other moving vehicle. No straight shortcut. No drift, skid, burnout, tyre smoke, wheelspin, hard braking, hydraulic hop, exaggerated bounce, scraping or sparks. No slow motion or speed ramp. Nothing conceals the change: no wipe, flash, white-out, flare, whip pan, smoke or opaque dust. Object stays visible every frame and never disappears then reappears. Its anchored centre and longitudinal direction never move; only geometry changes. No levitation, spinning or floating. @image3 and @image4 are never simultaneously complete. Final @image4 is plain BLACK only - never orange, gold-wheeled, futuristic or customised. No duplicates. @image4 never starts, rolls or turns its wheels. @image1 never approaches or touches it. No slow motion, speed ramp or freeze. No gimbal, dolly, crane, drone, zoom or rack focus. No glow, sparks, embers, lightning, beam, hologram, wireframe or portal. No relighting or change of time.

STRICT GEOGRAPHY: @image1 defines the location. @image2 remains exactly where the boombox appears in @image1, directly before the Viper's nose at the far-left curb. Its front panel faces outward toward the open cul-de-sac. @image3 crouches at SCREEN RIGHT of @image2, between the boombox and the open asphalt, close to the Viper's front corner. She does not crouch behind the box, between it and the car, or elsewhere in the cul-de-sac.

The Viper's nose, front wheel and low graphite fender occupy the upper-left background and remain visibly recognizable, proving the correct geography. The boombox dominates the lower-left foreground; the woman occupies frame right. The aerial and baseball bat remain separated and never intersect.

STRICT: EXACTLY ONE PERSON. No crew, neighbours, pedestrians, background figures or duplicates. Every vehicle in @image1 remains parked and motionless.

Shoulder-mounted handheld with soft operator weight: gentle breathing float, minute roll and tiny corrections. She looks only at the controls and keeps chewing.

1.5-1.9s The first track cuts out for a brief mechanical dropout. She releases pressure and the same button springs back to its original height. The boombox gives one almost invisible physical vibration but stays planted. No second button moves.

3.2-4.0s Still crouched on the same mark, she turns only her chin slightly toward the street while keeping the bat in her left hand. Her shoulders and knees remain fixed. She does not stand before the cut. The camera holds the close composition unchanged.

The button moves and returns on one axis; neighbouring buttons remain still. @image2 stays rigid and identical: speaker circles, cassette door, tuner, aerial, handle and scale never change. No sliding, rotation, floating or morphing. The Viper maintains fixed contact shadows and reflections.

ACTING: her eyes remain behind tinted lenses, so expression lives in jaw, mouth and chin. Small asymmetric chewing continues through all four seconds. Calm, practical and deadpan, not performing for anyone. Identity, skin, hairline, ponytail, clothing and sunglasses remain constant. Hair, fabric and aerial respond subtly to the same breeze.

LIGHT: match @image1 exactly. Warm sun grazes her skin and hair, Viper paint and boombox chrome; cool skylight retains shadow detail. Reflections stay physically tied to the location. No relighting or exposure pulse.

NOBODY SPEAKS. No dialogue, mouthing, ad-libs, mumbling, grunts, breaths-as-voice or laughs. Her lips never form words.

SFX - the first thin track already playing quietly, one isolated hard button click, a split-second audio dropout, the button's spring return, then a clearly different thin track from the boombox; sneakers on grit, fabric movement, wind, dry palm fronds and far traffic. BOTH TRACKS ARE DIEGETIC ONLY, small and localized inside the visible speakers. No soundtrack, score or full-range music bed.

## FULL PROMPT

6S · ONE TAKE · 2 REFERENCES

50mm Kodak 500T. cinematography. Low golden-hour sun raking across the asphalt, warm highlights, cool skylight in the shadows. Photorealistic live-action, raw documentary realism. No 3D render, no game engine, no CGI sheen. 24fps, 180-degree shutter, natural motion blur. SFX only no music. No subtitles. 6s. Scene context: Tight on the front wheel of a black 1960s coupe standing on a cracked residential street at golden hour. The car pulls away. The whole shot is one wheel going from dead still to rolling fast, and the road underneath it going from still to a blur. @image1 - the wheel and the car it belongs to: 1964 black hardtop coupe sitting low, chrome WIRE WHEEL with many thin polished spokes, chrome spinner cap at the centre, WHITEWALL tyre with a clean bright white sidewall band, black tyre wall, a sliver of black bodywork and chrome trim above it. @image2 - location: cracked pale asphalt with tar seams and painted marks, kerb, dry grit, cream stucco houses and palm trunks far behind, all out of focus. All cards give appearance ONLY. Do NOT reproduce sheet layout, multiple views, panels, captions or grey backdrop. STRICT: ONE WHEEL, ONE CAR. No people in frame at any point, no legs, no hands, no faces. No other vehicle. No second wheel in focus. CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS. The camera is HARD-MOUNTED to the car on a rigid arm, low down and just outside the front wheel, looking at it side-on from about a metre away. Because it is bolted to the car, the wheel stays in exactly the same place in frame for the entire shot and never drifts, while the ground behind and beneath it moves. Slight rigid vibration through the mount as the car rolls, no operator float, no reframing, no pan, no push-in, no rack focus. ACTION, TIMED: 0.0-1.5s Dead still. The wheel is stationary, every thin chrome

BOOTCAMP VS SLOP DAWGZ

65



<!-- page 66 -->

spoke individually sharp and separate, the whitewall band clean and unbroken, the tyre resting into the grit with a tight contact shadow. The asphalt behind is motionless. 1.5-3.0s It starts to turn. The spokes begin to sweep slowly - still countable, still readable one by one - and the whitewall band starts to rotate. The road behind begins to creep past. 3.0-4.5s Speed builds steadily. The spokes smear into a shimmering translucent disc, the whitewall becomes a solid clean white ring, and the asphalt behind the wheel starts to stream past with real motion blur. 4.5-6.0s Rolling fast now. The spokes are a smooth blurred disc with no individual spokes visible at all, the white ring is perfectly even, and the ground beneath is a hard horizontal streak. The tyre still meets the road with a correct rolling contact patch. MOTION IS PROVEN, NOT ASSERTED: the road surface, its tar seams and its painted marks stream past behind and under the wheel at a rate that matches the wheel's rotation; grit is thrown up in small amounts; the car's own shadow travels along the ground with it; the body settles slightly as it takes up drive. SFX - a deep V8 taking load and pulling away, tyre rubber gripping and rolling on cracked asphalt building steadily louder, grit ticking against the wheel arch, faint suspension movement, wind rising past the mic. No music. STRICT NEGATIVES: the spokes NEVER appear frozen, never strobe, never flicker, never rotate backwards, never step or stutter - they blur smoothly into a disc as speed rises. The wheel never leaves its position in frame, never spins in place, never smokes, never squeals, never slips. No people, legs, hands or faces. No cut, insert, second angle or wide shot revealing the whole car. No slow motion, no speed ramp, no freeze frame, no bullet time. No hero angle, no orbit around the wheel, no push-in, no zoom, no rack focus. No lens flare, no anamorphic streak. No music score or beat-synced editing. No relighting or change of time of day.

## FULL PROMPT

24S · ONE TAKE · 3 REFERENCES

28mm Kodak 500T. cinematography. Low golden-hour sun raking from frame right, long shadows, warm highlights, cool skylight in the shadows. Photorealistic live-action, raw documentary realism. No 3D render, no game engine, no CGI sheen. 24fps, 180-degree shutter, natural motion blur. SFX only no music. No subtitles. 24 seconds. Scene context: A stretch of LA road being resurfaced at golden hour. A rider comes past the camera, runs the length of the road with complete technical control, and clears the open trench where the surface has been dug out. Run, tricks, ramp, gap, landing and ride away all happen on one stretch of road in one unbroken shot. No circus - a rider who knows exactly what his machine does. @image1 - the rider: East Asian man, olive bomber jacket, dark jeans, plain matte-black full-face helmet with the dark visor DOWN throughout, face never visible. @image2 - his motorcycle: glossy black supersport, orange pinstripes, orange rim tape, titanium exhaust. @image3 - location, for surroundings and light: LA residential street, cream stucco houses behind chain-link fences, tall bare-trunk palms, poles and wires, dry verges, cracked asphalt, low golden sun. All cards give appearance ONLY. Do NOT reproduce sheet layout, multiple views, panels, captions or grey backdrop. STRICT: ONE RIDER, ONE MOTORCYCLE, ONE ROAD. No other people anywhere - no workmen, pedestrians or spectators. No other moving vehicles. No duplicates. THE ROAD - THE SAME PIECE OF ROAD FOR ALL 24 SECONDS, NEVER REBUILT OR REARRANGED: A long straight run of cracked asphalt with faded lane marks. Partway along, the surface has been stripped and replaced by a compacted DIRT RAMP of graded roadbase rising steadily to about a metre and a half, hard-packed, dusty, rutted, orange plastic barriers along one side. The ramp ends in a clean vertical broken lip of old asphalt. Beyond it a FIVE-METRE OPEN TRENCH cut across the full width of the road, exposed dirt and rubble in the bottom, nothing crossing it. Past the far lip the asphalt continues unbroken with more barriers and a long clear run-out. Ordinary municipal roadworks: dusty and mundane. CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS, NO SECOND ANGLE, ONE POSITION ONLY. The camera stands OFF TO THE SIDE of the road, low, at about wheel height, looking across in profile. It starts wide enough that the bike passes close to the lens, then STAYS THERE and lets the whole run happen left to right in front of it, holding ramp, gap and landing in the same frame. Shoulder-mounted handheld, soft operator weight - gentle float, small roll, tiny corrections. It PANS SLOWLY AND SMOOTHLY to follow him, never whipping, cutting, pushing in, racking focus, changing height or getting in front of him. ACTION, TIMED - ONE UNBROKEN RUN: 0.0-3.0s The bike comes past the lens close and low from frame LEFT at speed, wheels filling the near frame for a moment, then pulls away right. As it goes, the whole road opens up beyond it - run, dirt ramp, trench and landing all visible in the same frame. 3.0-7.0s CONTROLLED WHEELIE: he lifts the front wheel smoothly and holds it steady at a low constant height for several metres left to right, not wavering, then sets it down softly. No wobble, no fight, no drama. 7.0-10.0s ROLLING STOPPIE: he squeezes the front brake, the rear wheel lifts a short controlled distance while the bike keeps rolling forward held level, then comes down cleanly and he keeps moving. 10.0-15.0s He rolls on the throttle and ACCELERATES hard toward the ramp, body tucked, tyres roaring. 15.0-16.5s He runs up the dirt ramp, dust kicking off the rear tyre, and LAUNCHES off the broken asphalt lip. 16.5-18.5s FLIGHT: bike and rider cross the

BOOTCAMP VS SLOP DAWGZ 69

open trench in one arc, wheels turning in the air, suspension extended, front wheel slightly high, body still and centred. The whole arc from lip to landing happens inside this frame with the ground visible beneath him. 18.5-20.0s He LANDS on the far asphalt, front wheel then rear, suspension compressing hard and rebounding, tyres chirping and gripping, the bike settling straight. 20.0-24.0s He rides away RIGHT down the run-out, unhurried, not celebrating, not looking back, getting smaller in frame. The road is left dusty and empty behind him. PHYSICS: the bike has real mass. Both wheels stay on the ground except during the wheelie, the stoppie and the flight. Suspension compresses and extends visibly on the ramp and landing. Dust lifts off the dirt ramp and drifts; nothing explodes. Its shadow travels along the road throughout. Wheels turn visibly and the orange rim tape blurs into even rings, never strobing or frozen. SFX - engine passing close and Dopplering away, throttle rolling on and off, exhaust note rising and falling with each trick, tyres on cracked asphalt, front brake biting for the stoppie, engine picking up hard for the run-in, dirt under the tyres on the ramp, a moment of engine note with no tyre noise while airborne, a hard landing thump with suspension bottoming and tyres chirping, then the engine pulling away. Wind past the mic, palm fronds, far traffic. No music. STRICT NEGATIVES: no cut, insert, close-up, reverse angle or second camera position - one camera stays on one side of the road throughout. It never gets in front of the bike, rides with it, orbits, rises or goes aerial. No drone, crane or gimbal. No slow motion, speed ramp or freeze - the jump plays at full speed. No backflip, spin or stunt-show trick, only a low held wheelie, a short rolling stoppie and a clean gap jump. He never crashes, falls, wobbles out of control or puts a foot down. He never removes the helmet and his face is never visible. No wooden or metal stunt ramp, no plywood kicker - the ramp is compacted dirt from the roadworks. No sparks, fireballs, debris or smoke plume. No other people or vehicles. No lens flare timed to the jump. The road never changes shape, the trench never moves, the barriers never rearrange. No relighting.

Two street tricks back to back, moving left to right, no helmet, face visible. @image1 - the rider: Black man, black paisley headband tied round his head, full black beard, olive bomber with orange BC, white tee, silver chain, baggy dark jeans, white sneakers. (two per axle), black saddle, spoked wheels. @image3 - location, for surroundings and light: LA street, cracked pale asphalt, a low horizontal steel handrail about knee height running parallel to the road, a low flat concrete ledge further along, chain-link fence, dry grit, stucco buildings and palms behind, low golden sun. All cards give appearance ONLY. Do NOT reproduce sheet layout, multiple views, panels, captions or grey backdrop. STRICT: ONE PERSON, ONE BMX. No other people, no pedestrians, no other riders, no vehicles, no duplicates. He wears NO helmet and nothing on his head but the headband. CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS, ONE POSITION. 35mm, LOW, about knee height, off to the side looking across the run in profile so both the rail and the ledge sit inside the same frame. Shoulder-mounted handheld with real operator weight - a soft step-bounce, small roll, quick tiny corrections, never smooth or mechanical. It PANS to follow him left to right and lags a fraction behind the spin before catching up. No push-in, no zoom, no rack focus, no rise, no crane, no drone. ACTION, TIMED - TWO SEPARATE TRICKS, LEFT TO RIGHT, NEVER OVERLAPPING: 0.0-1.0s He enters from frame LEFT rolling fast, cranks twice and lines up alongside the low steel rail. 1.0-1.6s He hops up sideways onto the rail and lands ON HIS PEGS - the two pegs on one side of the bike sitting across the top of the rail, wheels off it entirely, bike locked parallel above the rail. 1.6-3.0s DOUBLE-PEG GRIND: he slides the full length of the rail balanced on those pegs, metal scraping on metal, arms out for balance, the bike held straight and level, sparks never appearing. 3.0-3.6s He reaches the end of the rail, hops off sideways and lands both wheels on the asphalt with a hard tyre slap, cranking once to keep his speed. 3.6-4.8s 180: he rolls up onto the low concrete ledge, compresses, springs off the edge and rotates HALF A TURN in the air, both wheels up, the bike coming round beneath him until he is facing back the way he came. 4.8-6.0s He LANDS backwards on the asphalt, rear wheel first, absorbs it through his knees and rolls away FAKIE - travelling in his original direction but facing the other way, looking back over his shoulder. PHYSICS: the bike has real weight. The pegs carry the whole load on the rail with a continuous grinding scrape. Tyres deform visibly on each landing and the rider absorbs impacts through bent knees and elbows. His shadow travels along the ground with him. Wheels turn visibly, spokes blurring into a disc, never strobing or frozen. The 180 is exactly half a rotation, no more and no less. ACTING: his eyes are fully visible with natural blinking, jaw set with concentration through the grind, then loose and easy once he rolls away. Not a stunt face, not a grimace, no performing to camera. Identity and skin texture stay constant. NOBODY SPEAKS. No dialogue, ad-libs, mumbling, grunts, whoops, breaths-as-voice or laughs. His lips never move to words. SFX - tyres on asphalt, freewheel ticking, chain under load, the hard metallic clack of pegs landing on the rail, a long continuous metal-on-metal grinding scrape, the drop off the end, tyres slapping down, a short scuff of rubber as he lands the spin. Wind past the mic, far city traffic. No music. STRICT NEGATIVES: no crash, no bail, no foot down, no slipping off the rail, no wobble out of control. No sparks at any point - steel pegs on a steel rail make no sparks here. No cut, insert, close-up, reverse angle or second camera position. The camera never gets in front of him, never rides with him, never orbits, never rises, never goes aerial. No drone, crane, gimbal or dolly. No slow motion, speed ramp, freeze frame or bullet time - everything plays at full speed. No full 360, no flip, no barspin, no tailwhip - only a double-peg grind and a half rotation. He never wears a helmet, never touches one, never has anything on his head but the headband. No other people or vehicles. No dust plumes or flying debris. No lens flare timed to the spin. He whips his shoulders round and pivots on the rear wheel, swinging himself back to forward-facing, and cranks twice for speed. 1.2-2.0s He drives at the stucco wall at a shallow angle, front wheel rising to meet it. 2.0-3.6s WALLRIDE: both tyres go ONTO THE VERTICAL WALL FACE about a metre above the ground and he carves a short arc along it, bike almost horizontal against the stucco, tyres pressed into the wall and gripping, body leaned out away from it, cranks still. It lasts only a couple of metres. 3.6-4.4s Gravity takes him. He comes off the wall and DROPS back down onto the asphalt, both wheels together, knees absorbing the landing, tyres slapping hard. 4.4-6.0s Without a pause he cranks twice more and cuts hard RIGHT into the narrow alley mouth, leaning the bike over, and disappears into its shade. The street is left empty in the low sun, dust drifting. PHYSICS: the bike has real weight. On the wall the tyres visibly deform against the stucco and there is real friction, not floating; his line curves under gravity from the moment he leaves the ground. Tyres deform again on the landing and the rider absorbs it through bent knees and elbows. His shadow travels along the ground and up the wall with him. Wheels turn visibly, spokes blurring into a disc, never strobing or frozen. The light changes naturally as he enters the alley: warm sun on the street, cool shade inside. ACTING: his eyes are fully visible with natural blinking, concentration through the wallride, easy and loose as he cuts into the alley. Not a stunt face, no performing to camera. Identity and skin texture stay constant. NOBODY SPEAKS. No dialogue, ad-libs, mumbling, grunts, whoops, breaths-as-voice or laughs. His lips never move to words. SFX - tyres on asphalt, freewheel ticking, chain under load, two hard cranks, rubber scrubbing on stucco during the wallride, both tyres slapping down on landing, then tyre noise changing as it enters the enclosed alley and echoes off the walls, receding. Wind past the mic, far city traffic. No music. STRICT NEGATIVES: no crash, no bail, no foot down, no slipping off the wall, no wobble out of control. The wallride is short and low - he never rides high up the

35mm Kodak 500T. handheld cinematography. Low golden-hour sun raking from frame right, long shadows, warm skin tones, cool skylight in the shadows. Photorealistic live-action, raw documentary realism. No 3D render, no game engine, no plastic skin. 24fps, 180-degree shutter, natural motion blur. SFX only no music. No subtitles. American accent. 8s. Scene context: A Los Angeles street at golden hour. A black coupe is cruising along at an easy pace. A man on a BMX comes up alongside the driver's window, pedalling to hold their speed, checks on them, and pulls away ahead. Everything in this shot is moving together at the same speed. @image1 - the rider: Black man, black paisley headband, full black beard, olive bomber with orange BC, white tee, silver chain, baggy dark jeans. No helmet, head bare except the headband, face fully visible. @image2 - his BMX: matte dark-green scuffed frame, black bars and cranks, four black pegs, spoked wheels. @image3 - the car: 1964 BLACK hardtop coupe, chrome bumpers and side spear, chrome side mirror, wire wheels with whitewalls, driver's window WOUND DOWN. @image4 - the driver, seen through the open window: man late twenties, olive skin, black hair with volume on top and a skin fade, dense black beard, glossy orange wraparound cat-eye sunglasses with amber lenses, black track jacket with off-white panels, both hands on the wheel. @image5 - the passenger, further inside the car and partly obscured: very large heavyset Black man, black bowler hat, thick black-rimmed glasses with clear lenses, olive shirt. @image6 - location, for surroundings and light: LA street, cracked pale asphalt, kerbs, chain-link fences, cream stucco houses, tall bare-trunk palms, poles and overhead wires, low golden sun. All cards give appearance ONLY. Do NOT reproduce sheet layout, multiple views, panels, captions or grey backdrop. STRICT: EXACTLY THREE PEOPLE. @image1 = ONLY the black headband and beard, on the BMX, outside the car. @image4 = ONLY the orange sunglasses, driving. @image5 = ONLY the bowler hat, in the passenger seat. No other people, no pedestrians, no other vehicles, no duplicates. CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS, NO SECOND ANGLE. 35mm, chest height, TRAVELLING ALONGSIDE the moving car and holding a two-shot of the open driver's window and the rider next to it. Shoulder-mounted handheld with real operator weight - a live float, small roll, tiny corrections, never mechanically smooth. It never pushes in, never racks focus, never drops back, never overtakes. EVERYTHING MOVES AS ONE SYSTEM: the car, the BMX and the camera all travel at exactly the same speed in the same direction, so relative to each other they stay locked in the same positions in frame throughout. THE ONLY THING THAT PROVES THEY ARE MOVING IS THE WORLD BEHIND THEM - kerbs, fences, palm trunks, poles and parked cars stream steadily past from frame right to frame left, near objects sweeping fast and far houses drifting slowly, while the car and rider hold still in frame. The car's own shadow travels along the road with it, the wire wheels turn visibly with their whitewalls blurred into even rings, and the BMX cranks turn as he pedals. ACTION, TIMED: 0.0-2.4s The car is already cruising. @image1 comes up from behind on the driver's side, cranking hard to catch it, and draws level with the open window, then settles into a steady pedalling rhythm to hold their pace. He rides one-handed for a moment, close to the door. 2.4-3.4s He leans in slightly toward the window and looks in at them. 3.4-4.4s @image1, easy, raised over the road noise: "You guys good?" 4.4-5.6s @image4 does not turn his head and does not answer, both hands staying on the wheel. Behind him @image5 lifts one thumb up where it can be seen through the window, and lets it drop again. 5.6-8.0s @image1 gives a short nod, puts his weight down on the pedals and PULLS AWAY forward, drawing ahead of the car and getting smaller as he goes, until only the empty road ahead is left in frame beside the window. Nobody understands why. He has an explanation. @image1 - the man getting in: Black man, black paisley headband, full black beard, olive bomber with orange BC, white tee, silver chain, baggy dark jeans, still out of breath. @image2 - the car: 1964 BLACK TWO-DOOR hardtop coupe, black pleated bench seats, thin large steering wheel, painted metal dash, chrome window winders. The driver's seatback is TIPPED FORWARD to let him through, because there are no rear doors. @image3 - already on the back bench, in the MIDDLE: very large heavyset Black man, black bowler hat, thick black-rimmed glasses with clear lenses, olive shirt with orange BC. He takes up most of the bench. @image4 - already on the back bench, on the FAR SIDE behind the empty front seat: blonde woman, dark roots, high long ponytail, narrow silver tinted sunglasses, olive crop top with orange BC, chewing gum. @image5 - the driver, front left, seen from behind and to the side: man late twenties, black hair with a skin fade, dense black beard, glossy orange wraparound cat-eye sunglasses, black track jacket with off-white panels. @image6 - location, seen through the windows: LA street, cracked asphalt, kerb, chain-link fence, cream stucco houses, palms, low golden sun. STRICT: EXACTLY FOUR PEOPLE. @image1 climbing in and ending up on the back bench. @image3 in the MIDDLE of the back bench. @image4 on the far side of the back bench. @image5 driving. CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS, NO SECOND ANGLE. 28mm, positioned outside the open driver's door looking in and slightly back, holding the door opening, the folded seatback, the empty front seat and the back bench all in the same frame. 28mm Kodak 500T. cinematography. Low golden-hour sun from the far side, hard backlight, long shadows, dust hanging in the air, cool shade under the concrete deck. Photorealistic live-action, raw documentary realism. No 3D render, no game engine, no CGI sheen. 24fps, 180-degree shutter, natural motion blur. SFX only no music. No subtitles. 12s. Scene context: A huge open concrete underpass beneath an elevated freeway in Los Angeles at the end of the day. Three people are already standing over on the right side, waiting. A black 1960s coupe comes in off the road, runs out across the open asphalt and puts itself sideways in one controlled slide, stopping square across from them. Nobody gets out yet. @image1 - the car: 1964 BLACK hardtop coupe, long flat hood, chrome bumpers and side spear, quad round tail lights, wire wheels with whitewall tyres, sitting low. Windows up, occupants not readable inside. @image2 - standing on the RIGHT, waiting: broad white man, spiked bleached blond hair, BURGUNDY paisley bandana round his head, narrow black sunglasses, BURGUNDY quilted sleeveless vest, white tee, beige cargo trousers. @image3 - beside him: Black man, long slouched black beanie, thin moustache and small chin beard, narrow dark sunglasses, BURGUNDY coach jacket over a long white tee, beige cargo trousers, a skateboard under one arm. @image4 - beside them: East Asian woman, high long black ponytail, nose ring, full colour tattoo sleeves and colour tattooed neck, BURGUNDY ribbed crop top, beige cargo shorts, black high-tops, a sheathed katana in her hand, standing next to a glossy dark burgundy supersport motorcycle on its side stand. @image5 - location: concrete overpass on massive square columns running overhead, wide open asphalt beneath with faded painted markings, sandy spoil banks and scrub, low white industrial buildings, poles, wires, palms and hazy hills far behind, low sun coming in under the deck in bars between the columns. All cards give appearance ONLY. Do NOT reproduce sheet layout, multiple views, panels, captions or grey backdrop. STRICT: EXACTLY THREE PEOPLE VISIBLE, ALL THREE ON THE RIGHT. @image2 = ONLY spiked blond hair and burgundy vest. @image3 = ONLY long beanie and skateboard. @image4 = ONLY black ponytail and sword. Nobody else is on foot anywhere in frame. Nobody gets out of the car. No extras, no crowd, no duplicates. CAMERA - ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO CUTS, NO SECOND ANGLE. Wide, 28mm, chest height at ground level, well back, holding the whole open arena: the empty asphalt where the car will come in across the LEFT and CENTRE, the three figures standing over in the RIGHT third, the columns marching across behind. Shoulder-mounted handheld with soft operator weight - slow live float, small roll, tiny corrections. It PANS SLOWLY to follow the car in and settles as it stops. It never pushes in, never racks focus, never cuts, never gets low and heroic. ACTION, TIMED: 0.0-2.0s The arena sits open and quiet. The three on the right are already standing there, still, backlit, watching the way in. A deep V8 is heard approaching from off frame left, still outside. 2.0-5.0s @image1 comes IN from frame LEFT, out of the light and into the shade under the deck, running across the open asphalt at a steady confident speed, its hard shadow travelling with it, dust lifting off the ground behind it. 5.0-7.5s CONTROLLED SLIDE: he lifts off, turns in and the back end steps out. The car rotates smoothly through about ninety degrees while still travelling, tyres scrubbing and smoking lightly, a long curved dust plume trailing off the rear wheels, and it comes to rest SIDEWAYS ON, broadside, facing across the arena directly at the three on the right, with a wide empty gap of asphalt left between them. 7.5-9.0s The body rocks once on its soft old suspension and settles. The dust plume keeps drifting past

PHYSICS: the slide is controlled and deliberate, never a skid out of control - the car keeps its line, rotates smoothly and stops exactly where it is aimed.
